So yes, I guess I do feel nervous or on edge a lot more than I used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there things you used to do that you're avoiding now because of how you've been feeling?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Not completely avoiding everything, but there are definitely things I’ve been putting off or staying away from more than I normally would.\n\nLike, around the house, I used to be pretty good about keeping up with little jobs—fixing something that needed fixing, making a call, dealing with paperwork, that kind of thing. Now I’ll see it sitting there and know it needs to be done, and I just… don’t start. I’ll tell myself I’ll get to it later, but later keeps turning into tomorrow, and then it piles up.\n\nAnd even things I used to do to clear my head, like going out to the workshop or trying to fish, I still do sometimes, but it doesn’t feel the same. I think part of me avoids it because I know I’ll just be out there with my thoughts spinning anyway. It used to be, if I was working with my hands or standing by the water, I could settle down. Now I’m still thinking about the tension at home, or replaying some stupid little argument I had with my wife over something that didn’t matter.\n\nSo yeah, I guess I’m avoiding responsibilities more, and even the things that used to help me don’t feel like much of an escape lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel sad for most of the day?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no—not most of the day. I mean, I have stretches where I feel down or kind of heavy, especially when I’m sitting with everything and thinking about how tense things have gotten, but I wouldn’t say I wake up sad and stay that way all day.\n\nIt’s more like it comes and goes. Some parts of the day I’m distracted or just doing whatever needs doing, and then other times it hits me that I’m not feeling very good about things. But “sad most of the day” sounds stronger than what I’d say I’m experiencing. I’m not sure if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you usually like to do?\n\nPatient: Not really the way I used to, no. I mean, I still try sometimes. I’ll go out to the workshop or think, “Okay, just go fishing for a while, clear your head,” because those were the things that usually helped me reset. But lately I get out there and it’s like my body is doing the thing, but my mind is still back in the house.\n\nEver since my father-in-law moved in, things just feel… crowded, tense, I guess. And I’ll be out in the workshop trying to focus on some small project, and instead I’m replaying how I snapped at my wife over something stupid, like some little house thing that didn’t really matter. Or I’m thinking about the jobs I haven’t done, paperwork I’ve been putting off, stuff I normally would’ve stayed on top of. So the enjoyment gets kind of swallowed up by all that.\n\nFishing used to be quiet for me. Now I’m standing there and my head still won’t quiet down. So I wouldn’t say I don’t like those things anymore, exactly. It’s more that they don’t give me the same relief or satisfaction they used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve noticed anything major, no. I mean, my clothes seem to fit about the same, as far as I can tell. I haven’t been getting on the scale regularly or anything, so I guess I can’t say for sure down to the pound.\n\nThere might be little ups and downs, but nothing where I’ve looked in the mirror or had to tighten my belt differently and thought, “Oh, something’s changed.” So… no, not really that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes where you suddenly felt extremely frightened or panicked?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not like that. I mean, I’ve definitely felt stressed and kind of overwhelmed at times, where I can feel myself getting worked up, but I wouldn’t describe it as suddenly being extremely frightened or panicked out of nowhere.\n\nWhen I think of panic, I think of something that comes on really fast, like you feel like something terrible is happening right that second, and I haven’t had that. Mine is more… tension building, I guess. Like I’ll be upset or on edge, but it’s connected to what’s going on, not some sudden attack of fear. So no, I don’t think I’ve had episodes like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a persistent sense of emptiness inside?\n\nPatient: Emptiness… I don’t know if I’d put it that way. I’ve felt off, and kind of worn down, but “empty inside” sounds a little more… I don’t know, hollow, like there’s nothing there, and I’m not sure that fits.\n\nI guess I’ve had moments where I’m sitting around and I don’t really know what to do with myself, or I’m not getting much out of things, but I wouldn’t say there’s this constant empty feeling inside me. It’s more like I’m preoccupied or weighed down. If you mean like a steady hollow feeling all the time, then no, I don’t think I’ve really noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest, exactly. I mean, there are things I used to enjoy that I maybe don’t get around to as much, or I’ll think, “I should do that,” and then I just don’t follow through. But it’s hard for me to tell whether that’s because I’m not interested anymore or just because I’ve been kind of distracted and not in the right frame of mind.\n\nIf I actually start doing something I normally like, I can usually still get something out of it. It just takes more effort to get myself going sometimes. So… I wouldn’t say the interest is gone, no. Maybe it’s been a little harder to access, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having thoughts or images that keep coming back to you, even when you try to push them away?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, though I don’t know if I’d call them “images” exactly. It’s more thoughts that keep looping. Like, I’ll be out in the workshop, trying to focus on something with my hands, or I’ll go fishing thinking it’ll clear my head, and instead my mind just keeps circling back to the same stuff at home.\n\nA lot of it started after my father-in-law moved in. I’ll keep replaying little things around the house, or things I said to my wife that I wish I hadn’t. I’ve been snapping at her over stupid house things, things that normally wouldn’t be that big of a deal, and then later I’m sitting there thinking about it over and over. Or I’ll remember some job or paperwork I’m putting off, and even if I tell myself, “Not now, just relax for a while,” it doesn’t really shut off.\n\nSo yeah, it’s not like some scary picture flashing in my mind, but I can’t seem to get my head quiet. The thoughts come back even when I’m deliberately trying to push them aside.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Sleep… I don’t know. I haven’t really been keeping track of it in any organized way, if that makes sense. Some nights I feel like I’m tossing around more than I used to, but then other nights I’m probably sleeping okay and just waking up not feeling especially refreshed. It’s hard for me to separate actual sleep trouble from just lying there thinking, or from normal bad nights.\n\nI wouldn’t want to say, “Yes, I’m not sleeping,” like it’s some clear pattern, because I’m not sure that’s true. I’ve noticed it a little here and there, but I haven’t really paid enough attention to say how often or how bad. So… maybe some restlessness at night, but I’m not sure I’d call it real trouble sleeping.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things you've said or done?\n\nPatient: Yeah… guilty, definitely. Worthless, I don’t know if I’d use that word exactly, but guilty, yes. Mostly about how I’ve been acting at home.\n\nEver since my father-in-law moved in, I feel like I’m not handling things the way I should. I’ll snap at my wife over some little house thing—something that probably doesn’t matter that much—and then afterward I’ll replay it and think, why did I say it like that? She doesn’t deserve that. It’s like I know I’m wound up, but in the moment I still let it come out sideways.\n\nAnd then there’s the other stuff, like jobs around the house or paperwork I used to just stay on top of. I put it off, and then I feel bad about that too, like I’m letting things pile up or making more stress for everybody. Even when I try to get away from it—go out to the workshop or go fishing—I’m still thinking about what I said, what I didn’t do, what I should be doing. So yeah, I wouldn’t say I feel worthless exactly, but I do feel guilty a lot about how I’ve been responding to everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Significant weight? No, I don’t think so. At least, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nI mean, I don’t weigh myself regularly or anything like that, so I can’t give you a number. My clothes seem to fit about the same, and nobody’s said anything to me like I look thinner. I guess my appetite can be a little all over the place depending on the day, but as far as actual weight loss, nothing that stands out to me as significant.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're worthless?\n\nPatient: Worthless… no, I don’t think that’s quite right. I know I said I feel guilty, and I do, but worthless feels like a stronger thing than what I’m feeling.\n\nI still feel like I’m me, if that makes sense. I may not be handling everything well, and I may look back on things I said and think, “That was lousy, I shouldn’t have done that,” but I don’t usually go from that to, “I’m worthless” or “I have no value.” It’s more like I’m disappointed in myself, or frustrated with how I reacted.\n\nSo… often? No, I wouldn’t say often. I can beat myself up some, but I don’t think I sit there feeling worthless.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Most of the time? I’m not sure I’d say that exactly. I do get down, but it’s not like I’m sad every minute of the day or walking around crying all the time or anything like that.

I mean, my clothes seem to fit about the same, as far as I can tell. I haven’t been getting on the scale regularly or anything, so I guess I can’t say for sure down to the pound.\n\nThere might be little ups and downs, but nothing where I’ve looked in the mirror or had to tighten my belt differently and thought, “Oh, something’s changed.” So… no, not really that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes where you suddenly felt extremely frightened or panicked?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not like that. I mean, I’ve definitely felt stressed and kind of overwhelmed at times, where I can feel myself getting worked up, but I wouldn’t describe it as suddenly being extremely frightened or panicked out of nowhere.\n\nWhen I think of panic, I think of something that comes on really fast, like you feel like something terrible is happening right that second, and I haven’t had that. Mine is more… tension building, I guess. Like I’ll be upset or on edge, but it’s connected to what’s going on, not some sudden attack of fear. So no, I don’t think I’ve had episodes like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a persistent sense of emptiness inside?\n\nPatient: Emptiness… I don’t know if I’d put it that way. I’ve felt off, and kind of worn down, but “empty inside” sounds a little more… I don’t know, hollow, like there’s nothing there, and I’m not sure that fits.\n\nI guess I’ve had moments where I’m sitting around and I don’t really know what to do with myself, or I’m not getting much out of things, but I wouldn’t say there’s this constant empty feeling inside me. It’s more like I’m preoccupied or weighed down. If you mean like a steady hollow feeling all the time, then no, I don’t think I’ve really noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest, exactly. I mean, there are things I used to enjoy that I maybe don’t get around to as much, or I’ll think, “I should do that,” and then I just don’t follow through. But it’s hard for me to tell whether that’s because I’m not interested anymore or just because I’ve been kind of distracted and not in the right frame of mind.\n\nIf I actually start doing something I normally like, I can usually still get something out of it. It just takes more effort to get myself going sometimes. So… I wouldn’t say the interest is gone, no. Maybe it’s been a little harder to access, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having thoughts or images that keep coming back to you, even when you try to push them away?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, though I don’t know if I’d call them “images” exactly. It’s more thoughts that keep looping. Like, I’ll be out in the workshop, trying to focus on something with my hands, or I’ll go fishing thinking it’ll clear my head, and instead my mind just keeps circling back to the same stuff at home.\n\nA lot of it started after my father-in-law moved in. I’ll keep replaying little things around the house, or things I said to my wife that I wish I hadn’t. I’ve been snapping at her over stupid house things, things that normally wouldn’t be that big of a deal, and then later I’m sitting there thinking about it over and over. Or I’ll remember some job or paperwork I’m putting off, and even if I tell myself, “Not now, just relax for a while,” it doesn’t really shut off.\n\nSo yeah, it’s not like some scary picture flashing in my mind, but I can’t seem to get my head quiet. The thoughts come back even when I’m deliberately trying to push them aside.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Sleep… I don’t know. I haven’t really been keeping track of it in any organized way, if that makes sense. Some nights I feel like I’m tossing around more than I used to, but then other nights I’m probably sleeping okay and just waking up not feeling especially refreshed. It’s hard for me to separate actual sleep trouble from just lying there thinking, or from normal bad nights.\n\nI wouldn’t want to say, “Yes, I’m not sleeping,” like it’s some clear pattern, because I’m not sure that’s true. I’ve noticed it a little here and there, but I haven’t really paid enough attention to say how often or how bad. So… maybe some restlessness at night, but I’m not sure I’d call it real trouble sleeping.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things you've said or done?\n\nPatient: Yeah… guilty, definitely. Worthless, I don’t know if I’d use that word exactly, but guilty, yes. Mostly about how I’ve been acting at home.\n\nEver since my father-in-law moved in, I feel like I’m not handling things the way I should. I’ll snap at my wife over some little house thing—something that probably doesn’t matter that much—and then afterward I’ll replay it and think, why did I say it like that? She doesn’t deserve that. It’s like I know I’m wound up, but in the moment I still let it come out sideways.\n\nAnd then there’s the other stuff, like jobs around the house or paperwork I used to just stay on top of. I put it off, and then I feel bad about that too, like I’m letting things pile up or making more stress for everybody. Even when I try to get away from it—go out to the workshop or go fishing—I’m still thinking about what I said, what I didn’t do, what I should be doing. So yeah, I wouldn’t say I feel worthless exactly, but I do feel guilty a lot about how I’ve been responding to everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Significant weight? No, I don’t think so. At least, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nI mean, I don’t weigh myself regularly or anything like that, so I can’t give you a number. My clothes seem to fit about the same, and nobody’s said anything to me like I look thinner. I guess my appetite can be a little all over the place depending on the day, but as far as actual weight loss, nothing that stands out to me as significant.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're worthless?\n\nPatient: Worthless… no, I don’t think that’s quite right. I know I said I feel guilty, and I do, but worthless feels like a stronger thing than what I’m feeling.\n\nI still feel like I’m me, if that makes sense. I may not be handling everything well, and I may look back on things I said and think, “That was lousy, I shouldn’t have done that,” but I don’t usually go from that to, “I’m worthless” or “I have no value.” It’s more like I’m disappointed in myself, or frustrated with how I reacted.\n\nSo… often? No, I wouldn’t say often. I can beat myself up some, but I don’t think I sit there feeling worthless.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad or down most of the time?\n\nPatient: Most of the time? I’m not sure I’d say that exactly. I do get down, but it’s not like I’m sad every minute of the day or walking around crying all the time or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more like… there are stretches where I feel kind of heavy or discouraged, especially when I’m thinking about how things have been going. But then there are other times where I’m just distracted, or I’m irritated, or I’m doing something and not really feeling sad so much as just worn out. So if you mean “sad most of the day, nearly every day,” I don’t know that I’d put it that strongly.\n\nI guess I’d say I’ve been more down than I usually am, but “most of the time” feels hard for me to answer. It comes and goes.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

I may not be handling everything well, and I may look back on things I said and think, “That was lousy, I shouldn’t have done that,” but I don’t usually go from that to, “I’m worthless” or “I have no value.” It’s more like I’m disappointed in myself, or frustrated with how I reacted.\n\nSo… often? No, I wouldn’t say often. I can beat myself up some, but I don’t think I sit there feeling worthless.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad or down most of the time?\n\nPatient: Most of the time? I’m not sure I’d say that exactly. I do get down, but it’s not like I’m sad every minute of the day or walking around crying all the time or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more like… there are stretches where I feel kind of heavy or discouraged, especially when I’m thinking about how things have been going. But then there are other times where I’m just distracted, or I’m irritated, or I’m doing something and not really feeling sad so much as just worn out. So if you mean “sad most of the day, nearly every day,” I don’t know that I’d put it that strongly.\n\nI guess I’d say I’ve been more down than I usually am, but “most of the time” feels hard for me to answer. It comes and goes.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you noticed a decrease in how much you enjoy the things you used to like doing?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 47 / not_in_workforce / some_college\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not totally sure. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’ve stopped enjoying everything or that nothing feels good anymore. It’s more complicated than that.

There are things I used to like doing that I still like once I actually get myself into them, but sometimes getting started feels like more effort than it should. Like, I might put something off or think, “Eh, I don’t feel like it,” but then if I do it, it’s not necessarily that I hate it or feel nothing. I can still enjoy parts of it.

So I guess… maybe a little less enthusiasm than usual, but I don’t know if I’d call it a real decrease in enjoyment. It’s not like all the pleasure is gone. It’s more that I’ve been kind of distracted or not as drawn to things lately, if that makes sense.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF41.1, F43.0, F32.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Yeah… well, I’m here because things at home have just gotten harder than I seem to be handling. Ever since my father-in-law moved in with us, I’ve been kind of… I don’t know, on edge all the time. I catch myself snapping at my wife over stupid little house things, stuff that normally wouldn’t be worth an argument at all.\n\nAnd I’ve been putting off things I usually stay on top of—jobs around the house, paperwork, just regular responsibilities. I’ll look at it and think, “I need to do that,” and then I just don’t. It piles up, and then I feel worse about it.\n\nThe thing that bothers me is I can’t seem to shut my head off. Even when I go out to the workshop or try to fish, which used to help me clear my mind, I’m still going over things in my head. It’s like I can’t get any quiet in there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed most of the time since your father-in-law moved in?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it sad or depressed most of the time, exactly. I mean, I’ve had moments where I feel pretty worn down by the whole situation, and I’m not exactly cheerful about how things are going at home. But when you say “depressed,” I think of something heavier, like being down all day every day, and I’m not sure that’s what this is.\n\nIt’s more like… I feel strained, if that makes sense. Like there’s this pressure in the house now, and I’m reacting to it more than I want to. So I wouldn’t say I’m walking around sad all the time, but I’m definitely not feeling like myself either.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or nervous?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think… nervous might be the word, or maybe wound up. I don’t always think of it as “anxiety,” exactly, but since my father-in-law moved in, I feel like I’m carrying this tension around the house all the time.\n\nIt shows up in little ways. I’ll snap at my wife over some small thing—like where something got put, or some household thing that really doesn’t deserve that kind of reaction—and then afterward I’m thinking, “Why did I make that into a whole thing?” And I’ll have jobs around the house or paperwork I normally would’ve just handled, but I keep putting it off, almost like I can’t get myself settled enough to start.\n\nEven when I try to get away from it a little, like going out to the workshop or trying to fish, my head doesn’t quiet down the way it used to. I’m still turning things over and over. So yes, I guess I do feel nervous or on edge a lot more than I used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there things you used to do that you're avoiding now because of how you've been feeling?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Not completely avoiding everything, but there are definitely things I’ve been putting off or staying away from more than I normally would.\n\nLike, around the house, I used to be pretty good about keeping up with little jobs—fixing something that needed fixing, making a call, dealing with paperwork, that kind of thing. Now I’ll see it sitting there and know it needs to be done, and I just… don’t start. I’ll tell myself I’ll get to it later, but later keeps turning into tomorrow, and then it piles up.\n\nAnd even things I used to do to clear my head, like going out to the workshop or trying to fish, I still do sometimes, but it doesn’t feel the same. I think part of me avoids it because I know I’ll just be out there with my thoughts spinning anyway. It used to be, if I was working with my hands or standing by the water, I could settle down. Now I’m still thinking about the tension at home, or replaying some stupid little argument I had with my wife over something that didn’t matter.\n\nSo yeah, I guess I’m avoiding responsibilities more, and even the things that used to help me don’t feel like much of an escape lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel sad for most of the day?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no—not most of the day. I mean, I have stretches where I feel down or kind of heavy, especially when I’m sitting with everything and thinking about how tense things have gotten, but I wouldn’t say I wake up sad and stay that way all day.\n\nIt’s more like it comes and goes. Some parts of the day I’m distracted or just doing whatever needs doing, and then other times it hits me that I’m not feeling very good about things. But “sad most of the day” sounds stronger than what I’d say I’m experiencing. I’m not sure if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you usually like to do?\n\nPatient: Not really the way I used to, no. I mean, I still try sometimes. I’ll go out to the workshop or think, “Okay, just go fishing for a while, clear your head,” because those were the things that usually helped me reset. But lately I get out there and it’s like my body is doing the thing, but my mind is still back in the house.\n\nEver since my father-in-law moved in, things just feel… crowded, tense, I guess. And I’ll be out in the workshop trying to focus on some small project, and instead I’m replaying how I snapped at my wife over something stupid, like some little house thing that didn’t really matter. Or I’m thinking about the jobs I haven’t done, paperwork I’ve been putting off, stuff I normally would’ve stayed on top of. So the enjoyment gets kind of swallowed up by all that.\n\nFishing used to be quiet for me. Now I’m standing there and my head still won’t quiet down. So I wouldn’t say I don’t like those things anymore, exactly. It’s more that they don’t give me the same relief or satisfaction they used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve noticed anything major, no. I mean, my clothes seem to fit about the same, as far as I can tell. I haven’t been getting on the scale regularly or anything, so I guess I can’t say for sure down to the pound.\n\nThere might be little ups and downs, but nothing where I’ve looked in the mirror or had to tighten my belt differently and thought, “Oh, something’s changed.” So… no, not really that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes where you suddenly felt extremely frightened or panicked?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not like that. I mean, I’ve definitely felt stressed and kind of overwhelmed at times, where I can feel myself getting worked up, but I wouldn’t describe it as suddenly being extremely frightened or panicked out of nowhere.\n\nWhen I think of panic, I think of something that comes on really fast, like you feel like something terrible is happening right that second, and I haven’t had that. Mine is more… tension building, I guess. Like I’ll be upset or on edge, but it’s connected to what’s going on, not some sudden attack of fear. So no, I don’t think I’ve had episodes like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a persistent sense of emptiness inside?\n\nPatient: Emptiness… I don’t know if I’d put it that way. I’ve felt off, and kind of worn down, but “empty inside” sounds a little more… I don’t know, hollow, like there’s nothing there, and I’m not sure that fits.\n\nI guess I’ve had moments where I’m sitting around and I don’t really know what to do with myself, or I’m not getting much out of things, but I wouldn’t say there’s this constant empty feeling inside me. It’s more like I’m preoccupied or weighed down. If you mean like a steady hollow feeling all the time, then no, I don’t think I’ve really noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest, exactly. I mean, there are things I used to enjoy that I maybe don’t get around to as much, or I’ll think, “I should do that,” and then I just don’t follow through. But it’s hard for me to tell whether that’s because I’m not interested anymore or just because I’ve been kind of distracted and not in the right frame of mind.\n\nIf I actually start doing something I normally like, I can usually still get something out of it. It just takes more effort to get myself going sometimes. So… I wouldn’t say the interest is gone, no. Maybe it’s been a little harder to access, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having thoughts or images that keep coming back to you, even when you try to push them away?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, though I don’t know if I’d call them “images” exactly. It’s more thoughts that keep looping. Like, I’ll be out in the workshop, trying to focus on something with my hands, or I’ll go fishing thinking it’ll clear my head, and instead my mind just keeps circling back to the same stuff at home.\n\nA lot of it started after my father-in-law moved in. I’ll keep replaying little things around the house, or things I said to my wife that I wish I hadn’t. I’ve been snapping at her over stupid house things, things that normally wouldn’t be that big of a deal, and then later I’m sitting there thinking about it over and over. Or I’ll remember some job or paperwork I’m putting off, and even if I tell myself, “Not now, just relax for a while,” it doesn’t really shut off.\n\nSo yeah, it’s not like some scary picture flashing in my mind, but I can’t seem to get my head quiet. The thoughts come back even when I’m deliberately trying to push them aside.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Sleep… I don’t know. I haven’t really been keeping track of it in any organized way, if that makes sense. Some nights I feel like I’m tossing around more than I used to, but then other nights I’m probably sleeping okay and just waking up not feeling especially refreshed. It’s hard for me to separate actual sleep trouble from just lying there thinking, or from normal bad nights.\n\nI wouldn’t want to say, “Yes, I’m not sleeping,” like it’s some clear pattern, because I’m not sure that’s true. I’ve noticed it a little here and there, but I haven’t really paid enough attention to say how often or how bad. So… maybe some restlessness at night, but I’m not sure I’d call it real trouble sleeping.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things you've said or done?\n\nPatient: Yeah… guilty, definitely. Worthless, I don’t know if I’d use that word exactly, but guilty, yes. Mostly about how I’ve been acting at home.\n\nEver since my father-in-law moved in, I feel like I’m not handling things the way I should. I’ll snap at my wife over some little house thing—something that probably doesn’t matter that much—and then afterward I’ll replay it and think, why did I say it like that? She doesn’t deserve that. It’s like I know I’m wound up, but in the moment I still let it come out sideways.\n\nAnd then there’s the other stuff, like jobs around the house or paperwork I used to just stay on top of. I put it off, and then I feel bad about that too, like I’m letting things pile up or making more stress for everybody. Even when I try to get away from it—go out to the workshop or go fishing—I’m still thinking about what I said, what I didn’t do, what I should be doing. So yeah, I wouldn’t say I feel worthless exactly, but I do feel guilty a lot about how I’ve been responding to everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Significant weight? No, I don’t think so. At least, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nI mean, I don’t weigh myself regularly or anything like that, so I can’t give you a number. My clothes seem to fit about the same, and nobody’s said anything to me like I look thinner. I guess my appetite can be a little all over the place depending on the day, but as far as actual weight loss, nothing that stands out to me as significant.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're worthless?\n\nPatient: Worthless… no, I don’t think that’s quite right. I know I said I feel guilty, and I do, but worthless feels like a stronger thing than what I’m feeling.\n\nI still feel like I’m me, if that makes sense. I may not be handling everything well, and I may look back on things I said and think, “That was lousy, I shouldn’t have done that,” but I don’t usually go from that to, “I’m worthless” or “I have no value.” It’s more like I’m disappointed in myself, or frustrated with how I reacted.\n\nSo… often? No, I wouldn’t say often. I can beat myself up some, but I don’t think I sit there feeling worthless.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad or down most of the time?\n\nPatient: Most of the time? I’m not sure I’d say that exactly. I do get down, but it’s not like I’m sad every minute of the day or walking around crying all the time or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more like… there are stretches where I feel kind of heavy or discouraged, especially when I’m thinking about how things have been going. But then there are other times where I’m just distracted, or I’m irritated, or I’m doing something and not really feeling sad so much as just worn out. It comes and goes.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in how much you enjoy the things you used to like doing?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’ve stopped enjoying everything or that nothing feels good anymore. It’s more complicated than that.\n\nThere are things I used to like doing that I still like once I actually get myself into them, but sometimes getting started feels like more effort than it should. Like, I might put something off or think, “Eh, I don’t feel like it,” but then if I do it, it’s not necessarily that I hate it or feel nothing. It’s more that I’ve been kind of distracted or not as drawn to things lately, if that makes sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. The thoughts come back even when I’m deliberately trying to push them aside.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Sleep… I don’t know. I haven’t really been keeping track of it in any organized way, if that makes sense. Some nights I feel like I’m tossing around more than I used to, but then other nights I’m probably sleeping okay and just waking up not feeling especially refreshed. It’s hard for me to separate actual sleep trouble from just lying there thinking, or from normal bad nights.\n\nI wouldn’t want to say, “Yes, I’m not sleeping,” like it’s some clear pattern, because I’m not sure that’s true. I’ve noticed it a little here and there, but I haven’t really paid enough attention to say how often or how bad. So… maybe some restlessness at night, but I’m not sure I’d call it real trouble sleeping.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things you've said or done?\n\nPatient: Yeah… guilty, definitely. Worthless, I don’t know if I’d use that word exactly, but guilty, yes. Mostly about how I’ve been acting at home.\n\nEver since my father-in-law moved in, I feel like I’m not handling things the way I should. I’ll snap at my wife over some little house thing—something that probably doesn’t matter that much—and then afterward I’ll replay it and think, why did I say it like that? She doesn’t deserve that. It’s like I know I’m wound up, but in the moment I still let it come out sideways.\n\nAnd then there’s the other stuff, like jobs around the house or paperwork I used to just stay on top of. I put it off, and then I feel bad about that too, like I’m letting things pile up or making more stress for everybody. Even when I try to get away from it—go out to the workshop or go fishing—I’m still thinking about what I said, what I didn’t do, what I should be doing. So yeah, I wouldn’t say I feel worthless exactly, but I do feel guilty a lot about how I’ve been responding to everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Significant weight? No, I don’t think so. At least, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nI mean, I don’t weigh myself regularly or anything like that, so I can’t give you a number. My clothes seem to fit about the same, and nobody’s said anything to me like I look thinner. I guess my appetite can be a little all over the place depending on the day, but as far as actual weight loss, nothing that stands out to me as significant.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're worthless?\n\nPatient: Worthless… no, I don’t think that’s quite right. I know I said I feel guilty, and I do, but worthless feels like a stronger thing than what I’m feeling.\n\nI still feel like I’m me, if that makes sense. I may not be handling everything well, and I may look back on things I said and think, “That was lousy, I shouldn’t have done that,” but I don’t usually go from that to, “I’m worthless” or “I have no value.” It’s more like I’m disappointed in myself, or frustrated with how I reacted.\n\nSo… often? No, I wouldn’t say often. I can beat myself up some, but I don’t think I sit there feeling worthless.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad or down most of the time?\n\nPatient: Most of the time? I’m not sure I’d say that exactly. I do get down, but it’s not like I’m sad every minute of the day or walking around crying all the time or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more like… there are stretches where I feel kind of heavy or discouraged, especially when I’m thinking about how things have been going. But then there are other times where I’m just distracted, or I’m irritated, or I’m doing something and not really feeling sad so much as just worn out. So if you mean “sad most of the day, nearly every day,” I don’t know that I’d put it that strongly.\n\nI guess I’d say I’ve been more down than I usually am, but “most of the time” feels hard for me to answer. It comes and goes.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in how much you enjoy the things you used to like doing?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’ve stopped enjoying everything or that nothing feels good anymore. It’s more complicated than that.\n\nThere are things I used to like doing that I still like once I actually get myself into them, but sometimes getting started feels like more effort than it should. Like, I might put something off or think, “Eh, I don’t feel like it,” but then if I do it, it’s not necessarily that I hate it or feel nothing. I can still enjoy parts of it.\n\nSo I guess… maybe a little less enthusiasm than usual, but I don’t know if I’d call it a real decrease in enjoyment. It’s not like all the pleasure is gone. It’s more that I’ve been kind of distracted or not as drawn to things lately, if that makes sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don’t really know how to answer that in a clean yes-or-no way. I mean, I’ve wondered about it, sure, because I don’t feel like myself in some ways. Even when I try to get away from it—go out to the workshop or go fishing—I’m still thinking about what I said, what I didn’t do, what I should be doing. So yeah, I wouldn’t say I feel worthless exactly, but I do feel guilty a lot about how I’ve been responding to everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Significant weight? No, I don’t think so. At least, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nI mean, I don’t weigh myself regularly or anything like that, so I can’t give you a number. My clothes seem to fit about the same, and nobody’s said anything to me like I look thinner. I guess my appetite can be a little all over the place depending on the day, but as far as actual weight loss, nothing that stands out to me as significant.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're worthless?\n\nPatient: Worthless… no, I don’t think that’s quite right. I know I said I feel guilty, and I do, but worthless feels like a stronger thing than what I’m feeling.\n\nI still feel like I’m me, if that makes sense. I may not be handling everything well, and I may look back on things I said and think, “That was lousy, I shouldn’t have done that,” but I don’t usually go from that to, “I’m worthless” or “I have no value.” It’s more like I’m disappointed in myself, or frustrated with how I reacted.\n\nSo… often? No, I wouldn’t say often. I can beat myself up some, but I don’t think I sit there feeling worthless.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad or down most of the time?\n\nPatient: Most of the time? I’m not sure I’d say that exactly. I do get down, but it’s not like I’m sad every minute of the day or walking around crying all the time or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more like… there are stretches where I feel kind of heavy or discouraged, especially when I’m thinking about how things have been going. But then there are other times where I’m just distracted, or I’m irritated, or I’m doing something and not really feeling sad so much as just worn out. So if you mean “sad most of the day, nearly every day,” I don’t know that I’d put it that strongly.\n\nI guess I’d say I’ve been more down than I usually am, but “most of the time” feels hard for me to answer. It comes and goes.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in how much you enjoy the things you used to like doing?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’ve stopped enjoying everything or that nothing feels good anymore. It’s more complicated than that.\n\nThere are things I used to like doing that I still like once I actually get myself into them, but sometimes getting started feels like more effort than it should. Like, I might put something off or think, “Eh, I don’t feel like it,” but then if I do it, it’s not necessarily that I hate it or feel nothing. I can still enjoy parts of it.\n\nSo I guess… maybe a little less enthusiasm than usual, but I don’t know if I’d call it a real decrease in enjoyment. It’s not like all the pleasure is gone. It’s more that I’ve been kind of distracted or not as drawn to things lately, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're depressed?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that in a clean yes-or-no way. I mean, I’ve wondered about it, sure, because I don’t feel like myself in some ways. I’m more down than I’m used to being, and I can get stuck thinking about things, but when I hear the word “depressed,” it sounds like… I don’t know, like this whole separate thing where everything is dark all the time.\n\nAnd I’m not sure that’s me. There are moments where I feel okay, or at least normal enough, and then there are moments where I feel pretty low. I mean, I’ve wondered about it, sure, because I don’t feel like myself in some ways. I’m more down than I’m used to being, and I can get stuck thinking about things, but when I hear the word “depressed,” it sounds like… I don’t know, like this whole separate thing where everything is dark all the time.\n\nAnd I’m not sure that’s me. There are moments where I feel okay, or at least normal enough, and then there are moments where I feel pretty low. So I guess I’d say I feel depressed sometimes, or I’ve been feeling down, but I don’t know if I’d say, “Yes, I’m depressed,” like I’m certain of that.\n\nPart of me also doesn’t want to overstate it. I know something’s off, and I know I haven’t been handling things the way I normally would, but I’m not sure what to call it.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you had at least two weeks where you felt sad or down most of the day?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 47 / not_in_workforce / some_college\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not sure I can say that clearly. Like, two weeks as a block where I was sad or down most of the day every day… I don’t know. I haven’t really been tracking it that neatly.

There have definitely been periods where I felt lower than usual, and some days where it kind of hung over me for a lot of the day. But then there are other days, or even parts of the same day, where I’m not exactly sad—I’m distracted, or just getting through things, or I feel sort of normal for a bit.

So if you’re asking whether there’s been a full two-week stretch where it was most of the day, nearly every day, I honestly don’t know that I could say yes. It’s been on and off more than that, at least from how I remember it.

    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF41.1, F43.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Yeah… well, I’m here because things at home have just gotten harder than I seem to be handling. Ever since my father-in-law moved in with us, I’ve been kind of… I don’t know, on edge all the time. I catch myself snapping at my wife over stupid little house things, stuff that normally wouldn’t be worth an argument at all.\n\nAnd I’ve been putting off things I usually stay on top of—jobs around the house, paperwork, just regular responsibilities. I’ll look at it and think, “I need to do that,” and then I just don’t. It piles up, and then I feel worse about it.\n\nThe thing that bothers me is I can’t seem to shut my head off. Even when I go out to the workshop or try to fish, which used to help me clear my mind, I’m still going over things in my head. It’s like I can’t get any quiet in there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed most of the time since your father-in-law moved in?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it sad or depressed most of the time, exactly. I mean, I’ve had moments where I feel pretty worn down by the whole situation, and I’m not exactly cheerful about how things are going at home. But when you say “depressed,” I think of something heavier, like being down all day every day, and I’m not sure that’s what this is.\n\nIt’s more like… I feel strained, if that makes sense. Like there’s this pressure in the house now, and I’m reacting to it more than I want to. So I wouldn’t say I’m walking around sad all the time, but I’m definitely not feeling like myself either.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or nervous?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think… nervous might be the word, or maybe wound up. I don’t always think of it as “anxiety,” exactly, but since my father-in-law moved in, I feel like I’m carrying this tension around the house all the time.\n\nIt shows up in little ways. I’ll snap at my wife over some small thing—like where something got put, or some household thing that really doesn’t deserve that kind of reaction—and then afterward I’m thinking, “Why did I make that into a whole thing?” And I’ll have jobs around the house or paperwork I normally would’ve just handled, but I keep putting it off, almost like I can’t get myself settled enough to start.\n\nEven when I try to get away from it a little, like going out to the workshop or trying to fish, my head doesn’t quiet down the way it used to. I’m still turning things over and over. So yes, I guess I do feel nervous or on edge a lot more than I used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there things you used to do that you're avoiding now because of how you've been feeling?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Not completely avoiding everything, but there are definitely things I’ve been putting off or staying away from more than I normally would.\n\nLike, around the house, I used to be pretty good about keeping up with little jobs—fixing something that needed fixing, making a call, dealing with paperwork, that kind of thing. Now I’ll see it sitting there and know it needs to be done, and I just… don’t start. I’ll tell myself I’ll get to it later, but later keeps turning into tomorrow, and then it piles up.\n\nAnd even things I used to do to clear my head, like going out to the workshop or trying to fish, I still do sometimes, but it doesn’t feel the same. I think part of me avoids it because I know I’ll just be out there with my thoughts spinning anyway. It used to be, if I was working with my hands or standing by the water, I could settle down. Now I’m still thinking about the tension at home, or replaying some stupid little argument I had with my wife over something that didn’t matter.\n\nSo yeah, I guess I’m avoiding responsibilities more, and even the things that used to help me don’t feel like much of an escape lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel sad for most of the day?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no—not most of the day. I mean, I have stretches where I feel down or kind of heavy, especially when I’m sitting with everything and thinking about how tense things have gotten, but I wouldn’t say I wake up sad and stay that way all day.\n\nIt’s more like it comes and goes. Some parts of the day I’m distracted or just doing whatever needs doing, and then other times it hits me that I’m not feeling very good about things. But “sad most of the day” sounds stronger than what I’d say I’m experiencing. I’m not sure if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you usually like to do?\n\nPatient: Not really the way I used to, no. I mean, I still try sometimes. I’ll go out to the workshop or think, “Okay, just go fishing for a while, clear your head,” because those were the things that usually helped me reset. But lately I get out there and it’s like my body is doing the thing, but my mind is still back in the house.\n\nEver since my father-in-law moved in, things just feel… crowded, tense, I guess. And I’ll be out in the workshop trying to focus on some small project, and instead I’m replaying how I snapped at my wife over something stupid, like some little house thing that didn’t really matter. Or I’m thinking about the jobs I haven’t done, paperwork I’ve been putting off, stuff I normally would’ve stayed on top of. So the enjoyment gets kind of swallowed up by all that.\n\nFishing used to be quiet for me. Now I’m standing there and my head still won’t quiet down. So I wouldn’t say I don’t like those things anymore, exactly. It’s more that they don’t give me the same relief or satisfaction they used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve noticed anything major, no. I mean, my clothes seem to fit about the same, as far as I can tell. I haven’t been getting on the scale regularly or anything, so I guess I can’t say for sure down to the pound.\n\nThere might be little ups and downs, but nothing where I’ve looked in the mirror or had to tighten my belt differently and thought, “Oh, something’s changed.” So… no, not really that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes where you suddenly felt extremely frightened or panicked?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not like that. I mean, I’ve definitely felt stressed and kind of overwhelmed at times, where I can feel myself getting worked up, but I wouldn’t describe it as suddenly being extremely frightened or panicked out of nowhere.\n\nWhen I think of panic, I think of something that comes on really fast, like you feel like something terrible is happening right that second, and I haven’t had that. Mine is more… tension building, I guess. Like I’ll be upset or on edge, but it’s connected to what’s going on, not some sudden attack of fear. So no, I don’t think I’ve had episodes like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a persistent sense of emptiness inside?\n\nPatient: Emptiness… I don’t know if I’d put it that way. I’ve felt off, and kind of worn down, but “empty inside” sounds a little more… I don’t know, hollow, like there’s nothing there, and I’m not sure that fits.\n\nI guess I’ve had moments where I’m sitting around and I don’t really know what to do with myself, or I’m not getting much out of things, but I wouldn’t say there’s this constant empty feeling inside me. It’s more like I’m preoccupied or weighed down. If you mean like a steady hollow feeling all the time, then no, I don’t think I’ve really noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest, exactly. I mean, there are things I used to enjoy that I maybe don’t get around to as much, or I’ll think, “I should do that,” and then I just don’t follow through. But it’s hard for me to tell whether that’s because I’m not interested anymore or just because I’ve been kind of distracted and not in the right frame of mind.\n\nIf I actually start doing something I normally like, I can usually still get something out of it. It just takes more effort to get myself going sometimes. So… I wouldn’t say the interest is gone, no. Maybe it’s been a little harder to access, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having thoughts or images that keep coming back to you, even when you try to push them away?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, though I don’t know if I’d call them “images” exactly. It’s more thoughts that keep looping. Like, I’ll be out in the workshop, trying to focus on something with my hands, or I’ll go fishing thinking it’ll clear my head, and instead my mind just keeps circling back to the same stuff at home.\n\nA lot of it started after my father-in-law moved in. I’ll keep replaying little things around the house, or things I said to my wife that I wish I hadn’t. I’ve been snapping at her over stupid house things, things that normally wouldn’t be that big of a deal, and then later I’m sitting there thinking about it over and over. Or I’ll remember some job or paperwork I’m putting off, and even if I tell myself, “Not now, just relax for a while,” it doesn’t really shut off.\n\nSo yeah, it’s not like some scary picture flashing in my mind, but I can’t seem to get my head quiet. The thoughts come back even when I’m deliberately trying to push them aside.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Sleep… I don’t know. I haven’t really been keeping track of it in any organized way, if that makes sense. Some nights I feel like I’m tossing around more than I used to, but then other nights I’m probably sleeping okay and just waking up not feeling especially refreshed. It’s hard for me to separate actual sleep trouble from just lying there thinking, or from normal bad nights.\n\nI wouldn’t want to say, “Yes, I’m not sleeping,” like it’s some clear pattern, because I’m not sure that’s true. I’ve noticed it a little here and there, but I haven’t really paid enough attention to say how often or how bad. So… maybe some restlessness at night, but I’m not sure I’d call it real trouble sleeping.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things you've said or done?\n\nPatient: Yeah… guilty, definitely. Worthless, I don’t know if I’d use that word exactly, but guilty, yes. Mostly about how I’ve been acting at home.\n\nEver since my father-in-law moved in, I feel like I’m not handling things the way I should. I’ll snap at my wife over some little house thing—something that probably doesn’t matter that much—and then afterward I’ll replay it and think, why did I say it like that? She doesn’t deserve that. It’s like I know I’m wound up, but in the moment I still let it come out sideways.\n\nAnd then there’s the other stuff, like jobs around the house or paperwork I used to just stay on top of. I put it off, and then I feel bad about that too, like I’m letting things pile up or making more stress for everybody. Even when I try to get away from it—go out to the workshop or go fishing—I’m still thinking about what I said, what I didn’t do, what I should be doing. So yeah, I wouldn’t say I feel worthless exactly, but I do feel guilty a lot about how I’ve been responding to everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Significant weight? No, I don’t think so. At least, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nI mean, I don’t weigh myself regularly or anything like that, so I can’t give you a number. My clothes seem to fit about the same, and nobody’s said anything to me like I look thinner. I guess my appetite can be a little all over the place depending on the day, but as far as actual weight loss, nothing that stands out to me as significant.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're worthless?\n\nPatient: Worthless… no, I don’t think that’s quite right. I know I said I feel guilty, and I do, but worthless feels like a stronger thing than what I’m feeling.\n\nI still feel like I’m me, if that makes sense. I may not be handling everything well, and I may look back on things I said and think, “That was lousy, I shouldn’t have done that,” but I don’t usually go from that to, “I’m worthless” or “I have no value.” It’s more like I’m disappointed in myself, or frustrated with how I reacted.\n\nSo… often? No, I wouldn’t say often. I can beat myself up some, but I don’t think I sit there feeling worthless.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad or down most of the time?\n\nPatient: Most of the time? I’m not sure I’d say that exactly. I do get down, but it’s not like I’m sad every minute of the day or walking around crying all the time or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more like… there are stretches where I feel kind of heavy or discouraged, especially when I’m thinking about how things have been going. But then there are other times where I’m just distracted, or I’m irritated, or I’m doing something and not really feeling sad so much as just worn out. So if you mean “sad most of the day, nearly every day,” I don’t know that I’d put it that strongly.\n\nI guess I’d say I’ve been more down than I usually am, but “most of the time” feels hard for me to answer. It comes and goes.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in how much you enjoy the things you used to like doing?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’ve stopped enjoying everything or that nothing feels good anymore. It’s more complicated than that.\n\nThere are things I used to like doing that I still like once I actually get myself into them, but sometimes getting started feels like more effort than it should. Like, I might put something off or think, “Eh, I don’t feel like it,” but then if I do it, it’s not necessarily that I hate it or feel nothing. I can still enjoy parts of it.\n\nSo I guess… maybe a little less enthusiasm than usual, but I don’t know if I’d call it a real decrease in enjoyment. It’s not like all the pleasure is gone. It’s more that I’ve been kind of distracted or not as drawn to things lately, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're depressed?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that in a clean yes-or-no way. I mean, I’ve wondered about it, sure, because I don’t feel like myself in some ways. I’m more down than I’m used to being, and I can get stuck thinking about things, but when I hear the word “depressed,” it sounds like… I don’t know, like this whole separate thing where everything is dark all the time.\n\nAnd I’m not sure that’s me. There are moments where I feel okay, or at least normal enough, and then there are moments where I feel pretty low. So I guess I’d say I feel depressed sometimes, or I’ve been feeling down, but I don’t know if I’d say, “Yes, I’m depressed,” like I’m certain of that.\n\nPart of me also doesn’t want to overstate it. I know something’s off, and I know I haven’t been handling things the way I normally would, but I’m not sure what to call it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had at least two weeks where you felt sad or down most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say that clearly. Like, two weeks as a block where I was sad or down most of the day every day… I don’t know. I haven’t really been tracking it that neatly.\n\nThere have definitely been periods where I felt lower than usual, and some days where it kind of hung over me for a lot of the day. But then there are other days, or even parts of the same day, where I’m not exactly sad—I’m distracted, or just getting through things, or I feel sort of normal for a bit.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether there’s been a full two-week stretch where it was most of the day, nearly every day, I honestly don’t know that I could say yes. It’s been on and off more than that, at least from how I remember it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

But it’s hard for me to tell whether that’s because I’m not interested anymore or just because I’ve been kind of distracted and not in the right frame of mind.\n\nIf I actually start doing something I normally like, I can usually still get something out of it. It just takes more effort to get myself going sometimes. So… I wouldn’t say the interest is gone, no. Maybe it’s been a little harder to access, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having thoughts or images that keep coming back to you, even when you try to push them away?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, though I don’t know if I’d call them “images” exactly. It’s more thoughts that keep looping. Like, I’ll be out in the workshop, trying to focus on something with my hands, or I’ll go fishing thinking it’ll clear my head, and instead my mind just keeps circling back to the same stuff at home.\n\nA lot of it started after my father-in-law moved in. I’ll keep replaying little things around the house, or things I said to my wife that I wish I hadn’t. I’ve been snapping at her over stupid house things, things that normally wouldn’t be that big of a deal, and then later I’m sitting there thinking about it over and over. Or I’ll remember some job or paperwork I’m putting off, and even if I tell myself, “Not now, just relax for a while,” it doesn’t really shut off.\n\nSo yeah, it’s not like some scary picture flashing in my mind, but I can’t seem to get my head quiet. The thoughts come back even when I’m deliberately trying to push them aside.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Sleep… I don’t know. I haven’t really been keeping track of it in any organized way, if that makes sense. Some nights I feel like I’m tossing around more than I used to, but then other nights I’m probably sleeping okay and just waking up not feeling especially refreshed. It’s hard for me to separate actual sleep trouble from just lying there thinking, or from normal bad nights.\n\nI wouldn’t want to say, “Yes, I’m not sleeping,” like it’s some clear pattern, because I’m not sure that’s true. I’ve noticed it a little here and there, but I haven’t really paid enough attention to say how often or how bad. So… maybe some restlessness at night, but I’m not sure I’d call it real trouble sleeping.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things you've said or done?\n\nPatient: Yeah… guilty, definitely. Worthless, I don’t know if I’d use that word exactly, but guilty, yes. Mostly about how I’ve been acting at home.\n\nEver since my father-in-law moved in, I feel like I’m not handling things the way I should. I’ll snap at my wife over some little house thing—something that probably doesn’t matter that much—and then afterward I’ll replay it and think, why did I say it like that? She doesn’t deserve that. It’s like I know I’m wound up, but in the moment I still let it come out sideways.\n\nAnd then there’s the other stuff, like jobs around the house or paperwork I used to just stay on top of. I put it off, and then I feel bad about that too, like I’m letting things pile up or making more stress for everybody. Even when I try to get away from it—go out to the workshop or go fishing—I’m still thinking about what I said, what I didn’t do, what I should be doing. So yeah, I wouldn’t say I feel worthless exactly, but I do feel guilty a lot about how I’ve been responding to everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Significant weight? No, I don’t think so. At least, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nI mean, I don’t weigh myself regularly or anything like that, so I can’t give you a number. My clothes seem to fit about the same, and nobody’s said anything to me like I look thinner. I guess my appetite can be a little all over the place depending on the day, but as far as actual weight loss, nothing that stands out to me as significant.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're worthless?\n\nPatient: Worthless… no, I don’t think that’s quite right. I know I said I feel guilty, and I do, but worthless feels like a stronger thing than what I’m feeling.\n\nI still feel like I’m me, if that makes sense. I may not be handling everything well, and I may look back on things I said and think, “That was lousy, I shouldn’t have done that,” but I don’t usually go from that to, “I’m worthless” or “I have no value.” It’s more like I’m disappointed in myself, or frustrated with how I reacted.\n\nSo… often? No, I wouldn’t say often. I can beat myself up some, but I don’t think I sit there feeling worthless.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad or down most of the time?\n\nPatient: Most of the time? I’m not sure I’d say that exactly. I do get down, but it’s not like I’m sad every minute of the day or walking around crying all the time or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more like… there are stretches where I feel kind of heavy or discouraged, especially when I’m thinking about how things have been going. But then there are other times where I’m just distracted, or I’m irritated, or I’m doing something and not really feeling sad so much as just worn out. So if you mean “sad most of the day, nearly every day,” I don’t know that I’d put it that strongly.\n\nI guess I’d say I’ve been more down than I usually am, but “most of the time” feels hard for me to answer. It comes and goes.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in how much you enjoy the things you used to like doing?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’ve stopped enjoying everything or that nothing feels good anymore. It’s more complicated than that.\n\nThere are things I used to like doing that I still like once I actually get myself into them, but sometimes getting started feels like more effort than it should. Like, I might put something off or think, “Eh, I don’t feel like it,” but then if I do it, it’s not necessarily that I hate it or feel nothing. I can still enjoy parts of it.\n\nSo I guess… maybe a little less enthusiasm than usual, but I don’t know if I’d call it a real decrease in enjoyment. It’s not like all the pleasure is gone. It’s more that I’ve been kind of distracted or not as drawn to things lately, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're depressed?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that in a clean yes-or-no way. I mean, I’ve wondered about it, sure, because I don’t feel like myself in some ways. I’m more down than I’m used to being, and I can get stuck thinking about things, but when I hear the word “depressed,” it sounds like… I don’t know, like this whole separate thing where everything is dark all the time.\n\nAnd I’m not sure that’s me. There are moments where I feel okay, or at least normal enough, and then there are moments where I feel pretty low. So I guess I’d say I feel depressed sometimes, or I’ve been feeling down, but I don’t know if I’d say, “Yes, I’m depressed,” like I’m certain of that.\n\nPart of me also doesn’t want to overstate it. I know something’s off, and I know I haven’t been handling things the way I normally would, but I’m not sure what to call it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had at least two weeks where you felt sad or down most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say that clearly. Like, two weeks as a block where I was sad or down most of the day every day… I don’t know. I haven’t really been tracking it that neatly.\n\nThere have definitely been periods where I felt lower than usual, and some days where it kind of hung over me for a lot of the day. But then there are other days, or even parts of the same day, where I’m not exactly sad—I’m distracted, or just getting through things, or I feel sort of normal for a bit.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether there’s been a full two-week stretch where it was most of the day, nearly every day, I honestly don’t know that I could say yes. It’s been on and off more than that, at least from how I remember it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
