But it’s hard for me to tell whether that’s because I’m not interested anymore or just because I’ve been kind of distracted and not in the right frame of mind.\n\nIf I actually start doing something I normally like, I can usually still get something out of it. It just takes more effort to get myself going sometimes. So… I wouldn’t say the interest is gone, no. Maybe it’s been a little harder to access, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having thoughts or images that keep coming back to you, even when you try to push them away?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, though I don’t know if I’d call them “images” exactly. It’s more thoughts that keep looping. Like, I’ll be out in the workshop, trying to focus on something with my hands, or I’ll go fishing thinking it’ll clear my head, and instead my mind just keeps circling back to the same stuff at home.\n\nA lot of it started after my father-in-law moved in. I’ll keep replaying little things around the house, or things I said to my wife that I wish I hadn’t. I’ve been snapping at her over stupid house things, things that normally wouldn’t be that big of a deal, and then later I’m sitting there thinking about it over and over. Or I’ll remember some job or paperwork I’m putting off, and even if I tell myself, “Not now, just relax for a while,” it doesn’t really shut off.\n\nSo yeah, it’s not like some scary picture flashing in my mind, but I can’t seem to get my head quiet. The thoughts come back even when I’m deliberately trying to push them aside.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Sleep… I don’t know. I haven’t really been keeping track of it in any organized way, if that makes sense. Some nights I feel like I’m tossing around more than I used to, but then other nights I’m probably sleeping okay and just waking up not feeling especially refreshed. It’s hard for me to separate actual sleep trouble from just lying there thinking, or from normal bad nights.\n\nI wouldn’t want to say, “Yes, I’m not sleeping,” like it’s some clear pattern, because I’m not sure that’s true. I’ve noticed it a little here and there, but I haven’t really paid enough attention to say how often or how bad. So… maybe some restlessness at night, but I’m not sure I’d call it real trouble sleeping.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things you've said or done?\n\nPatient: Yeah… guilty, definitely. Worthless, I don’t know if I’d use that word exactly, but guilty, yes. Mostly about how I’ve been acting at home.\n\nEver since my father-in-law moved in, I feel like I’m not handling things the way I should. I’ll snap at my wife over some little house thing—something that probably doesn’t matter that much—and then afterward I’ll replay it and think, why did I say it like that? She doesn’t deserve that. It’s like I know I’m wound up, but in the moment I still let it come out sideways.\n\nAnd then there’s the other stuff, like jobs around the house or paperwork I used to just stay on top of. I put it off, and then I feel bad about that too, like I’m letting things pile up or making more stress for everybody. Even when I try to get away from it—go out to the workshop or go fishing—I’m still thinking about what I said, what I didn’t do, what I should be doing. So yeah, I wouldn’t say I feel worthless exactly, but I do feel guilty a lot about how I’ve been responding to everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Significant weight? No, I don’t think so. At least, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nI mean, I don’t weigh myself regularly or anything like that, so I can’t give you a number. My clothes seem to fit about the same, and nobody’s said anything to me like I look thinner. I guess my appetite can be a little all over the place depending on the day, but as far as actual weight loss, nothing that stands out to me as significant.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're worthless?\n\nPatient: Worthless… no, I don’t think that’s quite right. I know I said I feel guilty, and I do, but worthless feels like a stronger thing than what I’m feeling.\n\nI still feel like I’m me, if that makes sense. I may not be handling everything well, and I may look back on things I said and think, “That was lousy, I shouldn’t have done that,” but I don’t usually go from that to, “I’m worthless” or “I have no value.” It’s more like I’m disappointed in myself, or frustrated with how I reacted.\n\nSo… often? No, I wouldn’t say often. I can beat myself up some, but I don’t think I sit there feeling worthless.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad or down most of the time?\n\nPatient: Most of the time? I’m not sure I’d say that exactly. I do get down, but it’s not like I’m sad every minute of the day or walking around crying all the time or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more like… there are stretches where I feel kind of heavy or discouraged, especially when I’m thinking about how things have been going. But then there are other times where I’m just distracted, or I’m irritated, or I’m doing something and not really feeling sad so much as just worn out. So if you mean “sad most of the day, nearly every day,” I don’t know that I’d put it that strongly.\n\nI guess I’d say I’ve been more down than I usually am, but “most of the time” feels hard for me to answer. It comes and goes.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in how much you enjoy the things you used to like doing?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’ve stopped enjoying everything or that nothing feels good anymore. It’s more complicated than that.\n\nThere are things I used to like doing that I still like once I actually get myself into them, but sometimes getting started feels like more effort than it should. Like, I might put something off or think, “Eh, I don’t feel like it,” but then if I do it, it’s not necessarily that I hate it or feel nothing. I can still enjoy parts of it.\n\nSo I guess… maybe a little less enthusiasm than usual, but I don’t know if I’d call it a real decrease in enjoyment. It’s not like all the pleasure is gone. It’s more that I’ve been kind of distracted or not as drawn to things lately, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're depressed?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that in a clean yes-or-no way. I mean, I’ve wondered about it, sure, because I don’t feel like myself in some ways. I’m more down than I’m used to being, and I can get stuck thinking about things, but when I hear the word “depressed,” it sounds like… I don’t know, like this whole separate thing where everything is dark all the time.\n\nAnd I’m not sure that’s me. There are moments where I feel okay, or at least normal enough, and then there are moments where I feel pretty low. So I guess I’d say I feel depressed sometimes, or I’ve been feeling down, but I don’t know if I’d say, “Yes, I’m depressed,” like I’m certain of that.\n\nPart of me also doesn’t want to overstate it. I know something’s off, and I know I haven’t been handling things the way I normally would, but I’m not sure what to call it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had at least two weeks where you felt sad or down most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say that clearly. Like, two weeks as a block where I was sad or down most of the day every day… I don’t know. I haven’t really been tracking it that neatly.\n\nThere have definitely been periods where I felt lower than usual, and some days where it kind of hung over me for a lot of the day. But then there are other days, or even parts of the same day, where I’m not exactly sad—I’m distracted, or just getting through things, or I feel sort of normal for a bit.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether there’s been a full two-week stretch where it was most of the day, nearly every day, I honestly don’t know that I could say yes.
